# Week 10 — Complete individual grayscale folio production

One individual 1080×1920 grayscale integrated illustrated memo folio: complete supplied headline, department heading, deck, narration and source-qualified lettering in the exact intended final composition. No annotation cards, miniature scene summaries, detached document exports, or generated six-up grids.

Image authority is explicit: current_appearance_override controls current face and costume; approved_lineup_crop supplies canonical identity; published_memo_precedent supplies only its stated layout/geometry application and never replaces current character anatomy or current scores. Follow the actual published memo references: one continuous cinematic world, prominent editorial hierarchy, slender ornament where appropriate, full narrative typography inside deliberate atmospheric reading space, and scene-led differences between departments. Grayscale affects palette, not the amount of copy, character identity, panel proportions, camera or final-page geometry.

SCENE-LED DESIGN RULES
- One complete 1080x1920 portrait folio may contain multiple deliberate camera views. The current visual_storytelling shot contract controls the sequence; a complete artwork is not limited to one shot.
- Preserve published typographic identity and department recognition; vary placement and scale with scene function.
- No default header/image/bottom-paragraph stack; typography and image are composed together.
- Keep complete source copy; compose space around it rather than cutting words to fit.
- Long prose is editorial typography, not implausibly engraved on tiny props.
- Use short in-world lettering only where physically plausible. Exact names, data, scores and tables are deterministically typeset, straight and legible.
- Identical department_heading and deck are displayed once; source fields are not interpreted as a command to repeat the same matchup line twice.
- Characters and props may cross decoration but never essential letters or data.
- Test full native page at 390px phone width; reflow the page rather than shrink the copy below readable scale.
- Canonical identity, appearance overrides, established geometry and verified final sports facts take precedence over composition novelty.
- All new personal quotes, jokes, anecdotes and factual claims require source/Founder input; an AI suggestion is not accepted canon.

SEQUENTIAL VISUAL STORYTELLING
- One complete 1080x1920 folio is the output artifact, not a rule requiring one camera view. Multiple shots/POV areas within that folio are explicitly authorized working direction.
- Use a visual sequence only when the additional view reveals new information: intention, cause, response or consequence. Do not pad with duplicate crops of the same pose.
- Distinguish simultaneous opposing POV from chronological action. Temporal cuts must not read as cloned characters or objects in one continuous space.
- Each action folio has a dominant image; subsidiary views earn their space. Avoid identical grids, mandatory equal splits and three strips on every page.
- Canon and exact factual lettering outrank novelty. Use each character’s current reference assets in every shot, preserve morphology, costume, companions and named location geometry.
- Preserve full publication copy once and in source order. Distribute it deliberately through the visual reading path; no additional mandatory caption below each image, no extra invented dialogue.
- A quiet master image and a level documentary table are intentional storytelling choices. Do not panelize every supporting page or turn Opening Drive into six miniature matchup posters.
- Match meaningful objects, material states and weather between pages. Editorial visual echoes do not prove physical adjacency, contemporaneity or character travel.
- Inspect each native folio and 390px reading proof for POV legibility, coherent cause, readable text and exact score before visual acceptance. Tests verify instructions only, never artistic success.
The visual_storytelling shot plan defines all within-page views. Existing camera/foreground fields describe the anchor scene, not a ban on supplementary or opposing views. Do not copy old paragraph-strip placement from a precedent.

  "headline": "ROOM ABOVE TWO HUNDRED",
  "deck": "The week the pressure reached everyone",
  "body": "By Sunday night, the Commonwealth had learned that a number could become a weapon without anyone lifting a blade. Two teams had crossed two hundred. One sent a hamburger to a birthday party; another brought an army to a mountain gate. A centaur was chasing an outlaw through Highland mud. In the eastern ruins, a river quietly made a mockery of Red Leopards’ sandbags, while a second gust carried the same storm's reach to a court farther inland. Seven Deadly Chins found the one household that needed quiet and arrived with a jackhammer. Slob on my Dobb made a beer delivery through a swamp whose owner could no longer command the road. The front belonged to El Niño; the humiliations belonged to the men who chose them. With four regular-season games now remaining, the question was no longer simply who won. It was what each result would leave behind.",

## W10-P03 — KEEP THE WHOLE LEAGUE IN VIEW

One individual 1080×1920 grayscale integrated illustrated memo folio: complete supplied headline, department heading, deck, narration and source-qualified lettering in the exact intended final composition. No annotation cards, miniature scene summaries, detached document exports, or generated six-up grids.

Image authority is explicit: current_appearance_override controls current face and costume; approved_lineup_crop supplies canonical identity; published_memo_precedent supplies only its stated layout/geometry application and never replaces current character anatomy or current scores. Follow the actual published memo references: one continuous cinematic world, prominent editorial hierarchy, slender ornament where appropriate, full narrative typography inside deliberate atmospheric reading space, and scene-led differences between departments. Grayscale affects palette, not the amount of copy, character identity, panel proportions, camera or final-page geometry.

SCENE-LED DESIGN RULES
- One complete 1080x1920 portrait folio may contain multiple deliberate camera views. The current visual_storytelling shot contract controls the sequence; a complete artwork is not limited to one shot.
- Preserve published typographic identity and department recognition; vary placement and scale with scene function.
- No default header/image/bottom-paragraph stack; typography and image are composed together.
- Keep complete source copy; compose space around it rather than cutting words to fit.
- Long prose is editorial typography, not implausibly engraved on tiny props.
- Use short in-world lettering only where physically plausible. Exact names, data, scores and tables are deterministically typeset, straight and legible.
- Identical department_heading and deck are displayed once; source fields are not interpreted as a command to repeat the same matchup line twice.
- Characters and props may cross decoration but never essential letters or data.
- Test full native page at 390px phone width; reflow the page rather than shrink the copy below readable scale.
- Canonical identity, appearance overrides, established geometry and verified final sports facts take precedence over composition novelty.
- All new personal quotes, jokes, anecdotes and factual claims require source/Founder input; an AI suggestion is not accepted canon.

SEQUENTIAL VISUAL STORYTELLING
- One complete 1080x1920 folio is the output artifact, not a rule requiring one camera view. Multiple shots/POV areas within that folio are explicitly authorized working direction.
- Use a visual sequence only when the additional view reveals new information: intention, cause, response or consequence. Do not pad with duplicate crops of the same pose.
- Distinguish simultaneous opposing POV from chronological action. Temporal cuts must not read as cloned characters or objects in one continuous space.
- Each action folio has a dominant image; subsidiary views earn their space. Avoid identical grids, mandatory equal splits and three strips on every page.
- Canon and exact factual lettering outrank novelty. Use each character’s current reference assets in every shot, preserve morphology, costume, companions and named location geometry.
- Preserve full publication copy once and in source order. Distribute it deliberately through the visual reading path; no additional mandatory caption below each image, no extra invented dialogue.
- A quiet master image and a level documentary table are intentional storytelling choices. Do not panelize every supporting page or turn Opening Drive into six miniature matchup posters.
- Match meaningful objects, material states and weather between pages. Editorial visual echoes do not prove physical adjacency, contemporaneity or character travel.
- Inspect each native folio and 390px reading proof for POV legibility, coherent cause, readable text and exact score before visual acceptance. Tests verify instructions only, never artistic success.
The visual_storytelling shot plan defines all within-page views. Existing camera/foreground fields describe the anchor scene, not a ban on supplementary or opposing views. Do not copy old paragraph-strip placement from a precedent.

{
  "headline": "KEEP THE WHOLE LEAGUE IN VIEW",
  "deck": "Commissioner’s note • After Week 10",
  "body": "There is a difference between being beaten and having to live with the evidence. A score is entered once. A birthday photograph is shown forever. A broken hoop stays broken until someone fixes it. The owner of a swamp still has to watch the next truck drive through. That is the Commonwealth we have spent seasons building, and it is why this league is more than six boxes on a scoreboard. But our stories do not outrank the standings. Nobody is going to Cancún by mathematical decree until the last route is closed. Nobody is healed because a reserve slot looks full. The numbers have settled, and six teams now share 6–4 records. The next week will ask each of us what we have left to field.",
  "callouts": [],
  "rows": [],
  "department_heading": "FROM THE COMMISSIONER"
}

{
  "headline": "Everybody Gets a View",
  "deck": "The Chili Cheesers vs D0nkey K0ng",
  "body": "The Chili Cheesers has always understood the value of a crowd. At last week's cookout he chose the victim, the serving and the moment everyone laughed. Now he circles the Arsenal's Highland ring with the crowd above him, searching for an exit while the four-legged centaur closes behind, lasso turning overhead. Every open gate seems to belong to D0nkey K0ng. The same storm that wet the rails has made the ground a poor place to sprint in expensive boots.",
  "department_heading": "The Chili Cheesers vs D0nkey K0ng"
}

## W10-P06 — The Ring Runs Out

One individual 1080×1920 grayscale integrated illustrated memo folio: complete supplied headline, department heading, deck, narration and source-qualified lettering in the exact intended final composition. No annotation cards, miniature scene summaries, detached document exports, or generated six-up grids.

Image authority is explicit: current_appearance_override controls current face and costume; approved_lineup_crop supplies canonical identity; published_memo_precedent supplies only its stated layout/geometry application and never replaces current character anatomy or current scores. Follow the actual published memo references: one continuous cinematic world, prominent editorial hierarchy, slender ornament where appropriate, full narrative typography inside deliberate atmospheric reading space, and scene-led differences between departments. Grayscale affects palette, not the amount of copy, character identity, panel proportions, camera or final-page geometry.

SCENE-LED DESIGN RULES
- One complete 1080x1920 portrait folio may contain multiple deliberate camera views. The current visual_storytelling shot contract controls the sequence; a complete artwork is not limited to one shot.
- Preserve published typographic identity and department recognition; vary placement and scale with scene function.
- No default header/image/bottom-paragraph stack; typography and image are composed together.
- Keep complete source copy; compose space around it rather than cutting words to fit.
- Long prose is editorial typography, not implausibly engraved on tiny props.
- Use short in-world lettering only where physically plausible. Exact names, data, scores and tables are deterministically typeset, straight and legible.
- Identical department_heading and deck are displayed once; source fields are not interpreted as a command to repeat the same matchup line twice.
- Characters and props may cross decoration but never essential letters or data.
- Test full native page at 390px phone width; reflow the page rather than shrink the copy below readable scale.
- Canonical identity, appearance overrides, established geometry and verified final sports facts take precedence over composition novelty.
- All new personal quotes, jokes, anecdotes and factual claims require source/Founder input; an AI suggestion is not accepted canon.

SEQUENTIAL VISUAL STORYTELLING
- One complete 1080x1920 folio is the output artifact, not a rule requiring one camera view. Multiple shots/POV areas within that folio are explicitly authorized working direction.
- Use a visual sequence only when the additional view reveals new information: intention, cause, response or consequence. Do not pad with duplicate crops of the same pose.
- Distinguish simultaneous opposing POV from chronological action. Temporal cuts must not read as cloned characters or objects in one continuous space.
- Each action folio has a dominant image; subsidiary views earn their space. Avoid identical grids, mandatory equal splits and three strips on every page.
- Canon and exact factual lettering outrank novelty. Use each character’s current reference assets in every shot, preserve morphology, costume, companions and named location geometry.
- Preserve full publication copy once and in source order. Distribute it deliberately through the visual reading path; no additional mandatory caption below each image, no extra invented dialogue.
- A quiet master image and a level documentary table are intentional storytelling choices. Do not panelize every supporting page or turn Opening Drive into six miniature matchup posters.
- Match meaningful objects, material states and weather between pages. Editorial visual echoes do not prove physical adjacency, contemporaneity or character travel.
- Inspect each native folio and 390px reading proof for POV legibility, coherent cause, readable text and exact score before visual acceptance. Tests verify instructions only, never artistic success.
The visual_storytelling shot plan defines all within-page views. Existing camera/foreground fields describe the anchor scene, not a ban on supplementary or opposing views. Do not copy old paragraph-strip placement from a precedent.

## W10-P07 — No Private Exit

One individual 1080×1920 grayscale integrated illustrated memo folio: complete supplied headline, department heading, deck, narration and source-qualified lettering in the exact intended final composition. No annotation cards, miniature scene summaries, detached document exports, or generated six-up grids.

Image authority is explicit: current_appearance_override controls current face and costume; approved_lineup_crop supplies canonical identity; published_memo_precedent supplies only its stated layout/geometry application and never replaces current character anatomy or current scores. Follow the actual published memo references: one continuous cinematic world, prominent editorial hierarchy, slender ornament where appropriate, full narrative typography inside deliberate atmospheric reading space, and scene-led differences between departments. Grayscale affects palette, not the amount of copy, character identity, panel proportions, camera or final-page geometry.

SCENE-LED DESIGN RULES
- One complete 1080x1920 portrait folio may contain multiple deliberate camera views. The current visual_storytelling shot contract controls the sequence; a complete artwork is not limited to one shot.
- Preserve published typographic identity and department recognition; vary placement and scale with scene function.
- No default header/image/bottom-paragraph stack; typography and image are composed together.
- Keep complete source copy; compose space around it rather than cutting words to fit.
- Long prose is editorial typography, not implausibly engraved on tiny props.
- Use short in-world lettering only where physically plausible. Exact names, data, scores and tables are deterministically typeset, straight and legible.
- Identical department_heading and deck are displayed once; source fields are not interpreted as a command to repeat the same matchup line twice.
- Characters and props may cross decoration but never essential letters or data.
- Test full native page at 390px phone width; reflow the page rather than shrink the copy below readable scale.
- Canonical identity, appearance overrides, established geometry and verified final sports facts take precedence over composition novelty.
- All new personal quotes, jokes, anecdotes and factual claims require source/Founder input; an AI suggestion is not accepted canon.

SEQUENTIAL VISUAL STORYTELLING
- One complete 1080x1920 folio is the output artifact, not a rule requiring one camera view. Multiple shots/POV areas within that folio are explicitly authorized working direction.
- Use a visual sequence only when the additional view reveals new information: intention, cause, response or consequence. Do not pad with duplicate crops of the same pose.
- Distinguish simultaneous opposing POV from chronological action. Temporal cuts must not read as cloned characters or objects in one continuous space.
- Each action folio has a dominant image; subsidiary views earn their space. Avoid identical grids, mandatory equal splits and three strips on every page.
- Canon and exact factual lettering outrank novelty. Use each character’s current reference assets in every shot, preserve morphology, costume, companions and named location geometry.
- Preserve full publication copy once and in source order. Distribute it deliberately through the visual reading path; no additional mandatory caption below each image, no extra invented dialogue.
- A quiet master image and a level documentary table are intentional storytelling choices. Do not panelize every supporting page or turn Opening Drive into six miniature matchup posters.
- Match meaningful objects, material states and weather between pages. Editorial visual echoes do not prove physical adjacency, contemporaneity or character travel.
- Inspect each native folio and 390px reading proof for POV legibility, coherent cause, readable text and exact score before visual acceptance. Tests verify instructions only, never artistic success.
The visual_storytelling shot plan defines all within-page views. Existing camera/foreground fields describe the anchor scene, not a ban on supplementary or opposing views. Do not copy old paragraph-strip placement from a precedent.

## W10-P08 — One More Bag

One individual 1080×1920 grayscale integrated illustrated memo folio: complete supplied headline, department heading, deck, narration and source-qualified lettering in the exact intended final composition. No annotation cards, miniature scene summaries, detached document exports, or generated six-up grids.

Image authority is explicit: current_appearance_override controls current face and costume; approved_lineup_crop supplies canonical identity; published_memo_precedent supplies only its stated layout/geometry application and never replaces current character anatomy or current scores. Follow the actual published memo references: one continuous cinematic world, prominent editorial hierarchy, slender ornament where appropriate, full narrative typography inside deliberate atmospheric reading space, and scene-led differences between departments. Grayscale affects palette, not the amount of copy, character identity, panel proportions, camera or final-page geometry.

SCENE-LED DESIGN RULES
- One complete 1080x1920 portrait folio may contain multiple deliberate camera views. The current visual_storytelling shot contract controls the sequence; a complete artwork is not limited to one shot.
- Preserve published typographic identity and department recognition; vary placement and scale with scene function.
- No default header/image/bottom-paragraph stack; typography and image are composed together.
- Keep complete source copy; compose space around it rather than cutting words to fit.
- Long prose is editorial typography, not implausibly engraved on tiny props.
- Use short in-world lettering only where physically plausible. Exact names, data, scores and tables are deterministically typeset, straight and legible.
- Identical department_heading and deck are displayed once; source fields are not interpreted as a command to repeat the same matchup line twice.
- Characters and props may cross decoration but never essential letters or data.
- Test full native page at 390px phone width; reflow the page rather than shrink the copy below readable scale.
- Canonical identity, appearance overrides, established geometry and verified final sports facts take precedence over composition novelty.
- All new personal quotes, jokes, anecdotes and factual claims require source/Founder input; an AI suggestion is not accepted canon.

SEQUENTIAL VISUAL STORYTELLING
- One complete 1080x1920 folio is the output artifact, not a rule requiring one camera view. Multiple shots/POV areas within that folio are explicitly authorized working direction.
- Use a visual sequence only when the additional view reveals new information: intention, cause, response or consequence. Do not pad with duplicate crops of the same pose.
- Distinguish simultaneous opposing POV from chronological action. Temporal cuts must not read as cloned characters or objects in one continuous space.
- Each action folio has a dominant image; subsidiary views earn their space. Avoid identical grids, mandatory equal splits and three strips on every page.
- Canon and exact factual lettering outrank novelty. Use each character’s current reference assets in every shot, preserve morphology, costume, companions and named location geometry.
- Preserve full publication copy once and in source order. Distribute it deliberately through the visual reading path; no additional mandatory caption below each image, no extra invented dialogue.
- A quiet master image and a level documentary table are intentional storytelling choices. Do not panelize every supporting page or turn Opening Drive into six miniature matchup posters.
- Match meaningful objects, material states and weather between pages. Editorial visual echoes do not prove physical adjacency, contemporaneity or character travel.
- Inspect each native folio and 390px reading proof for POV legibility, coherent cause, readable text and exact score before visual acceptance. Tests verify instructions only, never artistic success.
The visual_storytelling shot plan defines all within-page views. Existing camera/foreground fields describe the anchor scene, not a ban on supplementary or opposing views. Do not copy old paragraph-strip placement from a precedent.

{
  "headline": "One More Bag",
  "deck": "Red Leopards vs El Niño",
  "body": "The river did not arrive as a wall. It climbed a little, found a gap, and waited for Red Leopards to believe the last sandbag had solved it. The old goal leaned over a pitch still carrying last week's damage. Red Leopards shifted another bag into place. The water went around it. In 2022, he had beaten El Niño twice and carried thirteen wins into the semifinal. It was not the strength of the old season he remembered now, but how little of it had mattered on the night it ended.",
  "department_heading": "Red Leopards vs El Niño"
}

{
  "headline": "The Place That Answered Once",
  "deck": "Red Leopards vs El Niño",
  "body": "Later, in the Central Basin, Red Leopards reaches the old basketball court, a place associated with a different recent victory. The same expanding front has arrived by a different road. A gust twists the branches above the familiar rim. He has barely looked up when the old limb comes down across the backboard. One storm, two places, and no time to make either one safe.",
  "department_heading": "Red Leopards vs El Niño"
}

## W10-P10 — That Cold Again

One individual 1080×1920 grayscale integrated illustrated memo folio: complete supplied headline, department heading, deck, narration and source-qualified lettering in the exact intended final composition. No annotation cards, miniature scene summaries, detached document exports, or generated six-up grids.

Image authority is explicit: current_appearance_override controls current face and costume; approved_lineup_crop supplies canonical identity; published_memo_precedent supplies only its stated layout/geometry application and never replaces current character anatomy or current scores. Follow the actual published memo references: one continuous cinematic world, prominent editorial hierarchy, slender ornament where appropriate, full narrative typography inside deliberate atmospheric reading space, and scene-led differences between departments. Grayscale affects palette, not the amount of copy, character identity, panel proportions, camera or final-page geometry.

SCENE-LED DESIGN RULES
- One complete 1080x1920 portrait folio may contain multiple deliberate camera views. The current visual_storytelling shot contract controls the sequence; a complete artwork is not limited to one shot.
- Preserve published typographic identity and department recognition; vary placement and scale with scene function.
- No default header/image/bottom-paragraph stack; typography and image are composed together.
- Keep complete source copy; compose space around it rather than cutting words to fit.
- Long prose is editorial typography, not implausibly engraved on tiny props.
- Use short in-world lettering only where physically plausible. Exact names, data, scores and tables are deterministically typeset, straight and legible.
- Identical department_heading and deck are displayed once; source fields are not interpreted as a command to repeat the same matchup line twice.
- Characters and props may cross decoration but never essential letters or data.
- Test full native page at 390px phone width; reflow the page rather than shrink the copy below readable scale.
- Canonical identity, appearance overrides, established geometry and verified final sports facts take precedence over composition novelty.
- All new personal quotes, jokes, anecdotes and factual claims require source/Founder input; an AI suggestion is not accepted canon.

SEQUENTIAL VISUAL STORYTELLING
- One complete 1080x1920 folio is the output artifact, not a rule requiring one camera view. Multiple shots/POV areas within that folio are explicitly authorized working direction.
- Use a visual sequence only when the additional view reveals new information: intention, cause, response or consequence. Do not pad with duplicate crops of the same pose.
- Distinguish simultaneous opposing POV from chronological action. Temporal cuts must not read as cloned characters or objects in one continuous space.
- Each action folio has a dominant image; subsidiary views earn their space. Avoid identical grids, mandatory equal splits and three strips on every page.
- Canon and exact factual lettering outrank novelty. Use each character’s current reference assets in every shot, preserve morphology, costume, companions and named location geometry.
- Preserve full publication copy once and in source order. Distribute it deliberately through the visual reading path; no additional mandatory caption below each image, no extra invented dialogue.
- A quiet master image and a level documentary table are intentional storytelling choices. Do not panelize every supporting page or turn Opening Drive into six miniature matchup posters.
- Match meaningful objects, material states and weather between pages. Editorial visual echoes do not prove physical adjacency, contemporaneity or character travel.
- Inspect each native folio and 390px reading proof for POV legibility, coherent cause, readable text and exact score before visual acceptance. Tests verify instructions only, never artistic success.
The visual_storytelling shot plan defines all within-page views. Existing camera/foreground fields describe the anchor scene, not a ban on supplementary or opposing views. Do not copy old paragraph-strip placement from a precedent.

  "body": "The hoop hangs wrong. Red Leopards sits beneath it with the mud of his failed sandbag line still on him. ## W10-P11 — THE ROADS BETWEEN

One individual 1080×1920 grayscale integrated illustrated memo folio: complete supplied headline, department heading, deck, narration and source-qualified lettering in the exact intended final composition. No annotation cards, miniature scene summaries, detached document exports, or generated six-up grids.

Image authority is explicit: current_appearance_override controls current face and costume; approved_lineup_crop supplies canonical identity; published_memo_precedent supplies only its stated layout/geometry application and never replaces current character anatomy or current scores. Follow the actual published memo references: one continuous cinematic world, prominent editorial hierarchy, slender ornament where appropriate, full narrative typography inside deliberate atmospheric reading space, and scene-led differences between departments. Grayscale affects palette, not the amount of copy, character identity, panel proportions, camera or final-page geometry.

SCENE-LED DESIGN RULES
- One complete 1080x1920 portrait folio may contain multiple deliberate camera views. The current visual_storytelling shot contract controls the sequence; a complete artwork is not limited to one shot.
- Preserve published typographic identity and department recognition; vary placement and scale with scene function.
- No default header/image/bottom-paragraph stack; typography and image are composed together.
- Keep complete source copy; compose space around it rather than cutting words to fit.
- Long prose is editorial typography, not implausibly engraved on tiny props.
- Use short in-world lettering only where physically plausible. Exact names, data, scores and tables are deterministically typeset, straight and legible.
- Identical department_heading and deck are displayed once; source fields are not interpreted as a command to repeat the same matchup line twice.
- Characters and props may cross decoration but never essential letters or data.
- Test full native page at 390px phone width; reflow the page rather than shrink the copy below readable scale.
- Canonical identity, appearance overrides, established geometry and verified final sports facts take precedence over composition novelty.
- All new personal quotes, jokes, anecdotes and factual claims require source/Founder input; an AI suggestion is not accepted canon.

SEQUENTIAL VISUAL STORYTELLING
- One complete 1080x1920 folio is the output artifact, not a rule requiring one camera view. Multiple shots/POV areas within that folio are explicitly authorized working direction.
- Use a visual sequence only when the additional view reveals new information: intention, cause, response or consequence. Do not pad with duplicate crops of the same pose.
- Distinguish simultaneous opposing POV from chronological action. Temporal cuts must not read as cloned characters or objects in one continuous space.
- Each action folio has a dominant image; subsidiary views earn their space. Avoid identical grids, mandatory equal splits and three strips on every page.
- Canon and exact factual lettering outrank novelty. Use each character’s current reference assets in every shot, preserve morphology, costume, companions and named location geometry.
- Preserve full publication copy once and in source order. Distribute it deliberately through the visual reading path; no additional mandatory caption below each image, no extra invented dialogue.
- A quiet master image and a level documentary table are intentional storytelling choices. Do not panelize every supporting page or turn Opening Drive into six miniature matchup posters.
- Match meaningful objects, material states and weather between pages. Editorial visual echoes do not prove physical adjacency, contemporaneity or character travel.
- Inspect each native folio and 390px reading proof for POV legibility, coherent cause, readable text and exact score before visual acceptance. Tests verify instructions only, never artistic success.
The visual_storytelling shot plan defines all within-page views. Existing camera/foreground fields describe the anchor scene, not a ban on supplementary or opposing views. Do not copy old paragraph-strip placement from a precedent.

## W10-P14 — No Quiet Hours

One individual 1080×1920 grayscale integrated illustrated memo folio: complete supplied headline, department heading, deck, narration and source-qualified lettering in the exact intended final composition. No annotation cards, miniature scene summaries, detached document exports, or generated six-up grids.

Image authority is explicit: current_appearance_override controls current face and costume; approved_lineup_crop supplies canonical identity; published_memo_precedent supplies only its stated layout/geometry application and never replaces current character anatomy or current scores. Follow the actual published memo references: one continuous cinematic world, prominent editorial hierarchy, slender ornament where appropriate, full narrative typography inside deliberate atmospheric reading space, and scene-led differences between departments. Grayscale affects palette, not the amount of copy, character identity, panel proportions, camera or final-page geometry.

SCENE-LED DESIGN RULES
- One complete 1080x1920 portrait folio may contain multiple deliberate camera views. The current visual_storytelling shot contract controls the sequence; a complete artwork is not limited to one shot.
- Preserve published typographic identity and department recognition; vary placement and scale with scene function.
- No default header/image/bottom-paragraph stack; typography and image are composed together.
- Keep complete source copy; compose space around it rather than cutting words to fit.
- Long prose is editorial typography, not implausibly engraved on tiny props.
- Use short in-world lettering only where physically plausible. Exact names, data, scores and tables are deterministically typeset, straight and legible.
- Identical department_heading and deck are displayed once; source fields are not interpreted as a command to repeat the same matchup line twice.
- Characters and props may cross decoration but never essential letters or data.
- Test full native page at 390px phone width; reflow the page rather than shrink the copy below readable scale.
- Canonical identity, appearance overrides, established geometry and verified final sports facts take precedence over composition novelty.
- All new personal quotes, jokes, anecdotes and factual claims require source/Founder input; an AI suggestion is not accepted canon.

SEQUENTIAL VISUAL STORYTELLING
- One complete 1080x1920 folio is the output artifact, not a rule requiring one camera view. Multiple shots/POV areas within that folio are explicitly authorized working direction.
- Use a visual sequence only when the additional view reveals new information: intention, cause, response or consequence. Do not pad with duplicate crops of the same pose.
- Distinguish simultaneous opposing POV from chronological action. Temporal cuts must not read as cloned characters or objects in one continuous space.
- Each action folio has a dominant image; subsidiary views earn their space. Avoid identical grids, mandatory equal splits and three strips on every page.
- Canon and exact factual lettering outrank novelty. Use each character’s current reference assets in every shot, preserve morphology, costume, companions and named location geometry.
- Preserve full publication copy once and in source order. Distribute it deliberately through the visual reading path; no additional mandatory caption below each image, no extra invented dialogue.
- A quiet master image and a level documentary table are intentional storytelling choices. Do not panelize every supporting page or turn Opening Drive into six miniature matchup posters.
- Match meaningful objects, material states and weather between pages. Editorial visual echoes do not prove physical adjacency, contemporaneity or character travel.
- Inspect each native folio and 390px reading proof for POV legibility, coherent cause, readable text and exact score before visual acceptance. Tests verify instructions only, never artistic success.
The visual_storytelling shot plan defines all within-page views. Existing camera/foreground fields describe the anchor scene, not a ban on supplementary or opposing views. Do not copy old paragraph-strip placement from a precedent.

## W10-P15 — The Swamp Declares a Blockade

One individual 1080×1920 grayscale integrated illustrated memo folio: complete supplied headline, department heading, deck, narration and source-qualified lettering in the exact intended final composition. No annotation cards, miniature scene summaries, detached document exports, or generated six-up grids.

Image authority is explicit: current_appearance_override controls current face and costume; approved_lineup_crop supplies canonical identity; published_memo_precedent supplies only its stated layout/geometry application and never replaces current character anatomy or current scores. Follow the actual published memo references: one continuous cinematic world, prominent editorial hierarchy, slender ornament where appropriate, full narrative typography inside deliberate atmospheric reading space, and scene-led differences between departments. Grayscale affects palette, not the amount of copy, character identity, panel proportions, camera or final-page geometry.

SCENE-LED DESIGN RULES
- One complete 1080x1920 portrait folio may contain multiple deliberate camera views. The current visual_storytelling shot contract controls the sequence; a complete artwork is not limited to one shot.
- Preserve published typographic identity and department recognition; vary placement and scale with scene function.
- No default header/image/bottom-paragraph stack; typography and image are composed together.
- Keep complete source copy; compose space around it rather than cutting words to fit.
- Long prose is editorial typography, not implausibly engraved on tiny props.
- Use short in-world lettering only where physically plausible. Exact names, data, scores and tables are deterministically typeset, straight and legible.
- Identical department_heading and deck are displayed once; source fields are not interpreted as a command to repeat the same matchup line twice.
- Characters and props may cross decoration but never essential letters or data.
- Test full native page at 390px phone width; reflow the page rather than shrink the copy below readable scale.
- Canonical identity, appearance overrides, established geometry and verified final sports facts take precedence over composition novelty.
- All new personal quotes, jokes, anecdotes and factual claims require source/Founder input; an AI suggestion is not accepted canon.

SEQUENTIAL VISUAL STORYTELLING
- One complete 1080x1920 folio is the output artifact, not a rule requiring one camera view. Multiple shots/POV areas within that folio are explicitly authorized working direction.
- Use a visual sequence only when the additional view reveals new information: intention, cause, response or consequence. Do not pad with duplicate crops of the same pose.
- Distinguish simultaneous opposing POV from chronological action. Temporal cuts must not read as cloned characters or objects in one continuous space.
- Each action folio has a dominant image; subsidiary views earn their space. Avoid identical grids, mandatory equal splits and three strips on every page.
- Canon and exact factual lettering outrank novelty. Use each character’s current reference assets in every shot, preserve morphology, costume, companions and named location geometry.
- Preserve full publication copy once and in source order. Distribute it deliberately through the visual reading path; no additional mandatory caption below each image, no extra invented dialogue.
- A quiet master image and a level documentary table are intentional storytelling choices. Do not panelize every supporting page or turn Opening Drive into six miniature matchup posters.
- Match meaningful objects, material states and weather between pages. Editorial visual echoes do not prove physical adjacency, contemporaneity or character travel.
- Inspect each native folio and 390px reading proof for POV legibility, coherent cause, readable text and exact score before visual acceptance. Tests verify instructions only, never artistic success.
The visual_storytelling shot plan defines all within-page views. Existing camera/foreground fields describe the anchor scene, not a ban on supplementary or opposing views. Do not copy old paragraph-strip placement from a precedent.

  "deck": "Mud Dogs vs Slob on my Dobb",
  "body": "Mud Dogs plants himself beside the old swamp entrance marker, blocking the raised service strip with the confidence of a landlord. - Canonical identity, appearance overrides, established geometry and verified final sports facts take precedence over composition novelty.
- All new personal quotes, jokes, anecdotes and factual claims require source/Founder input; an AI suggestion is not accepted canon.

## W10-P18 — WHY THE BOARD MOVED

One individual 1080×1920 grayscale integrated illustrated memo folio: complete supplied headline, department heading, deck, narration and source-qualified lettering in the exact intended final composition. No annotation cards, miniature scene summaries, detached document exports, or generated six-up grids.

Image authority is explicit: current_appearance_override controls current face and costume; approved_lineup_crop supplies canonical identity; published_memo_precedent supplies only its stated layout/geometry application and never replaces current character anatomy or current scores. Follow the actual published memo references: one continuous cinematic world, prominent editorial hierarchy, slender ornament where appropriate, full narrative typography inside deliberate atmospheric reading space, and scene-led differences between departments. Grayscale affects palette, not the amount of copy, character identity, panel proportions, camera or final-page geometry.

SCENE-LED DESIGN RULES
- One complete 1080x1920 portrait folio may contain multiple deliberate camera views. The current visual_storytelling shot contract controls the sequence; a complete artwork is not limited to one shot.
- Preserve published typographic identity and department recognition; vary placement and scale with scene function.
- No default header/image/bottom-paragraph stack; typography and image are composed together.
- Keep complete source copy; compose space around it rather than cutting words to fit.
- Long prose is editorial typography, not implausibly engraved on tiny props.
- Use short in-world lettering only where physically plausible. Exact names, data, scores and tables are deterministically typeset, straight and legible.
- Identical department_heading and deck are displayed once; source fields are not interpreted as a command to repeat the same matchup line twice.
- Characters and props may cross decoration but never essential letters or data.
- Test full native page at 390px phone width; reflow the page rather than shrink the copy below readable scale.
- Canonical identity, appearance overrides, established geometry and verified final sports facts take precedence over composition novelty.
- All new personal quotes, jokes, anecdotes and factual claims require source/Founder input; an AI suggestion is not accepted canon.

SEQUENTIAL VISUAL STORYTELLING
- One complete 1080x1920 folio is the output artifact, not a rule requiring one camera view. Multiple shots/POV areas within that folio are explicitly authorized working direction.
- Use a visual sequence only when the additional view reveals new information: intention, cause, response or consequence. Do not pad with duplicate crops of the same pose.
- Distinguish simultaneous opposing POV from chronological action. Temporal cuts must not read as cloned characters or objects in one continuous space.
- Each action folio has a dominant image; subsidiary views earn their space. Avoid identical grids, mandatory equal splits and three strips on every page.
- Canon and exact factual lettering outrank novelty. Use each character’s current reference assets in every shot, preserve morphology, costume, companions and named location geometry.
- Preserve full publication copy once and in source order. Distribute it deliberately through the visual reading path; no additional mandatory caption below each image, no extra invented dialogue.
- A quiet master image and a level documentary table are intentional storytelling choices. Do not panelize every supporting page or turn Opening Drive into six miniature matchup posters.
- Match meaningful objects, material states and weather between pages. Editorial visual echoes do not prove physical adjacency, contemporaneity or character travel.
- Inspect each native folio and 390px reading proof for POV legibility, coherent cause, readable text and exact score before visual acceptance. Tests verify instructions only, never artistic success.
The visual_storytelling shot plan defines all within-page views. Existing camera/foreground fields describe the anchor scene, not a ban on supplementary or opposing views. Do not copy old paragraph-strip placement from a precedent.

## W10-P19 — A Very Generous Guest

One individual 1080×1920 grayscale integrated illustrated memo folio: complete supplied headline, department heading, deck, narration and source-qualified lettering in the exact intended final composition. No annotation cards, miniature scene summaries, detached document exports, or generated six-up grids.

Image authority is explicit: current_appearance_override controls current face and costume; approved_lineup_crop supplies canonical identity; published_memo_precedent supplies only its stated layout/geometry application and never replaces current character anatomy or current scores. Follow the actual published memo references: one continuous cinematic world, prominent editorial hierarchy, slender ornament where appropriate, full narrative typography inside deliberate atmospheric reading space, and scene-led differences between departments. Grayscale affects palette, not the amount of copy, character identity, panel proportions, camera or final-page geometry.

SCENE-LED DESIGN RULES
- One complete 1080x1920 portrait folio may contain multiple deliberate camera views. The current visual_storytelling shot contract controls the sequence; a complete artwork is not limited to one shot.
- Preserve published typographic identity and department recognition; vary placement and scale with scene function.
- No default header/image/bottom-paragraph stack; typography and image are composed together.
- Keep complete source copy; compose space around it rather than cutting words to fit.
- Long prose is editorial typography, not implausibly engraved on tiny props.
- Use short in-world lettering only where physically plausible. Exact names, data, scores and tables are deterministically typeset, straight and legible.
- Identical department_heading and deck are displayed once; source fields are not interpreted as a command to repeat the same matchup line twice.
- Characters and props may cross decoration but never essential letters or data.
- Test full native page at 390px phone width; reflow the page rather than shrink the copy below readable scale.
- Canonical identity, appearance overrides, established geometry and verified final sports facts take precedence over composition novelty.
- All new personal quotes, jokes, anecdotes and factual claims require source/Founder input; an AI suggestion is not accepted canon.

SEQUENTIAL VISUAL STORYTELLING
- One complete 1080x1920 folio is the output artifact, not a rule requiring one camera view. Multiple shots/POV areas within that folio are explicitly authorized working direction.
- Use a visual sequence only when the additional view reveals new information: intention, cause, response or consequence. Do not pad with duplicate crops of the same pose.
- Distinguish simultaneous opposing POV from chronological action. Temporal cuts must not read as cloned characters or objects in one continuous space.
- Each action folio has a dominant image; subsidiary views earn their space. Avoid identical grids, mandatory equal splits and three strips on every page.
- Canon and exact factual lettering outrank novelty. Use each character’s current reference assets in every shot, preserve morphology, costume, companions and named location geometry.
- Preserve full publication copy once and in source order. Distribute it deliberately through the visual reading path; no additional mandatory caption below each image, no extra invented dialogue.
- A quiet master image and a level documentary table are intentional storytelling choices. Do not panelize every supporting page or turn Opening Drive into six miniature matchup posters.
- Match meaningful objects, material states and weather between pages. Editorial visual echoes do not prove physical adjacency, contemporaneity or character travel.
- Inspect each native folio and 390px reading proof for POV legibility, coherent cause, readable text and exact score before visual acceptance. Tests verify instructions only, never artistic success.
The visual_storytelling shot plan defines all within-page views. Existing camera/foreground fields describe the anchor scene, not a ban on supplementary or opposing views. Do not copy old paragraph-strip placement from a precedent.

## W10-P20 — The 200 Burger

One individual 1080×1920 grayscale integrated illustrated memo folio: complete supplied headline, department heading, deck, narration and source-qualified lettering in the exact intended final composition. No annotation cards, miniature scene summaries, detached document exports, or generated six-up grids.

Image authority is explicit: current_appearance_override controls current face and costume; approved_lineup_crop supplies canonical identity; published_memo_precedent supplies only its stated layout/geometry application and never replaces current character anatomy or current scores. Follow the actual published memo references: one continuous cinematic world, prominent editorial hierarchy, slender ornament where appropriate, full narrative typography inside deliberate atmospheric reading space, and scene-led differences between departments. Grayscale affects palette, not the amount of copy, character identity, panel proportions, camera or final-page geometry.

SCENE-LED DESIGN RULES
- One complete 1080x1920 portrait folio may contain multiple deliberate camera views. The current visual_storytelling shot contract controls the sequence; a complete artwork is not limited to one shot.
- Preserve published typographic identity and department recognition; vary placement and scale with scene function.
- No default header/image/bottom-paragraph stack; typography and image are composed together.
- Keep complete source copy; compose space around it rather than cutting words to fit.
- Long prose is editorial typography, not implausibly engraved on tiny props.
- Use short in-world lettering only where physically plausible. Exact names, data, scores and tables are deterministically typeset, straight and legible.
- Identical department_heading and deck are displayed once; source fields are not interpreted as a command to repeat the same matchup line twice.
- Characters and props may cross decoration but never essential letters or data.
- Test full native page at 390px phone width; reflow the page rather than shrink the copy below readable scale.
- Canonical identity, appearance overrides, established geometry and verified final sports facts take precedence over composition novelty.
- All new personal quotes, jokes, anecdotes and factual claims require source/Founder input; an AI suggestion is not accepted canon.

SEQUENTIAL VISUAL STORYTELLING
- One complete 1080x1920 folio is the output artifact, not a rule requiring one camera view. Multiple shots/POV areas within that folio are explicitly authorized working direction.
- Use a visual sequence only when the additional view reveals new information: intention, cause, response or consequence. Do not pad with duplicate crops of the same pose.
- Distinguish simultaneous opposing POV from chronological action. Temporal cuts must not read as cloned characters or objects in one continuous space.
- Each action folio has a dominant image; subsidiary views earn their space. Avoid identical grids, mandatory equal splits and three strips on every page.
- Canon and exact factual lettering outrank novelty. Use each character’s current reference assets in every shot, preserve morphology, costume, companions and named location geometry.
- Preserve full publication copy once and in source order. Distribute it deliberately through the visual reading path; no additional mandatory caption below each image, no extra invented dialogue.
- A quiet master image and a level documentary table are intentional storytelling choices. Do not panelize every supporting page or turn Opening Drive into six miniature matchup posters.
- Match meaningful objects, material states and weather between pages. Editorial visual echoes do not prove physical adjacency, contemporaneity or character travel.
- Inspect each native folio and 390px reading proof for POV legibility, coherent cause, readable text and exact score before visual acceptance. Tests verify instructions only, never artistic success.
The visual_storytelling shot plan defines all within-page views. Existing camera/foreground fields describe the anchor scene, not a ban on supplementary or opposing views. Do not copy old paragraph-strip placement from a precedent.

EXACT FULL PUBLICATION COPY
{
  "headline": "The 200 Burger",
  "deck": "Dr. Duckhook vs His Majesty's Blood",
  "body": "Dr. Duckhook pulls the ribbon, raises the lid and discovers a double-decker hamburger big enough to have a title: 200 BURGER. For a moment he tries to smile like a gracious host. Then the guests understand what the number means. His Majesty's Blood has given him a celebration he can neither refuse nor explain away. The separate birthday cake waits on the sideboard.",
  "department_heading": "Dr. Duckhook vs His Majesty's Blood"
}

SCENE-LED DESIGN RULES

## W10-P23 — Two Ways Through

One individual 1080×1920 grayscale integrated illustrated memo folio: complete supplied headline, department heading, deck, narration and source-qualified lettering in the exact intended final composition. No annotation cards, miniature scene summaries, detached document exports, or generated six-up grids.

Image authority is explicit: current_appearance_override controls current face and costume; approved_lineup_crop supplies canonical identity; published_memo_precedent supplies only its stated layout/geometry application and never replaces current character anatomy or current scores. Follow the actual published memo references: one continuous cinematic world, prominent editorial hierarchy, slender ornament where appropriate, full narrative typography inside deliberate atmospheric reading space, and scene-led differences between departments. Grayscale affects palette, not the amount of copy, character identity, panel proportions, camera or final-page geometry.

SCENE-LED DESIGN RULES
- One complete 1080x1920 portrait folio may contain multiple deliberate camera views. The current visual_storytelling shot contract controls the sequence; a complete artwork is not limited to one shot.
- Preserve published typographic identity and department recognition; vary placement and scale with scene function.
- No default header/image/bottom-paragraph stack; typography and image are composed together.
- Keep complete source copy; compose space around it rather than cutting words to fit.
- Long prose is editorial typography, not implausibly engraved on tiny props.
- Use short in-world lettering only where physically plausible. Exact names, data, scores and tables are deterministically typeset, straight and legible.
- Identical department_heading and deck are displayed once; source fields are not interpreted as a command to repeat the same matchup line twice.
- Characters and props may cross decoration but never essential letters or data.
- Test full native page at 390px phone width; reflow the page rather than shrink the copy below readable scale.
- Canonical identity, appearance overrides, established geometry and verified final sports facts take precedence over composition novelty.
- All new personal quotes, jokes, anecdotes and factual claims require source/Founder input; an AI suggestion is not accepted canon.

SEQUENTIAL VISUAL STORYTELLING
- One complete 1080x1920 folio is the output artifact, not a rule requiring one camera view. Multiple shots/POV areas within that folio are explicitly authorized working direction.
- Use a visual sequence only when the additional view reveals new information: intention, cause, response or consequence. Do not pad with duplicate crops of the same pose.
- Distinguish simultaneous opposing POV from chronological action. Temporal cuts must not read as cloned characters or objects in one continuous space.
- Each action folio has a dominant image; subsidiary views earn their space. Avoid identical grids, mandatory equal splits and three strips on every page.
- Canon and exact factual lettering outrank novelty. Use each character’s current reference assets in every shot, preserve morphology, costume, companions and named location geometry.
- Preserve full publication copy once and in source order. Distribute it deliberately through the visual reading path; no additional mandatory caption below each image, no extra invented dialogue.
- A quiet master image and a level documentary table are intentional storytelling choices. Do not panelize every supporting page or turn Opening Drive into six miniature matchup posters.
- Match meaningful objects, material states and weather between pages. Editorial visual echoes do not prove physical adjacency, contemporaneity or character travel.
- Inspect each native folio and 390px reading proof for POV legibility, coherent cause, readable text and exact score before visual acceptance. Tests verify instructions only, never artistic success.
The visual_storytelling shot plan defines all within-page views. Existing camera/foreground fields describe the anchor scene, not a ban on supplementary or opposing views. Do not copy old paragraph-strip placement from a precedent.

## W10-P24 — Unlimited Firepower

One individual 1080×1920 grayscale integrated illustrated memo folio: complete supplied headline, department heading, deck, narration and source-qualified lettering in the exact intended final composition. No annotation cards, miniature scene summaries, detached document exports, or generated six-up grids.

Image authority is explicit: current_appearance_override controls current face and costume; approved_lineup_crop supplies canonical identity; published_memo_precedent supplies only its stated layout/geometry application and never replaces current character anatomy or current scores. Follow the actual published memo references: one continuous cinematic world, prominent editorial hierarchy, slender ornament where appropriate, full narrative typography inside deliberate atmospheric reading space, and scene-led differences between departments. Grayscale affects palette, not the amount of copy, character identity, panel proportions, camera or final-page geometry.

SCENE-LED DESIGN RULES
- One complete 1080x1920 portrait folio may contain multiple deliberate camera views. The current visual_storytelling shot contract controls the sequence; a complete artwork is not limited to one shot.
- Preserve published typographic identity and department recognition; vary placement and scale with scene function.
- No default header/image/bottom-paragraph stack; typography and image are composed together.
- Keep complete source copy; compose space around it rather than cutting words to fit.
- Long prose is editorial typography, not implausibly engraved on tiny props.
- Use short in-world lettering only where physically plausible. Exact names, data, scores and tables are deterministically typeset, straight and legible.
- Identical department_heading and deck are displayed once; source fields are not interpreted as a command to repeat the same matchup line twice.
- Characters and props may cross decoration but never essential letters or data.
- Test full native page at 390px phone width; reflow the page rather than shrink the copy below readable scale.
- Canonical identity, appearance overrides, established geometry and verified final sports facts take precedence over composition novelty.
- All new personal quotes, jokes, anecdotes and factual claims require source/Founder input; an AI suggestion is not accepted canon.

SEQUENTIAL VISUAL STORYTELLING
- One complete 1080x1920 folio is the output artifact, not a rule requiring one camera view. Multiple shots/POV areas within that folio are explicitly authorized working direction.
- Use a visual sequence only when the additional view reveals new information: intention, cause, response or consequence. Do not pad with duplicate crops of the same pose.
- Distinguish simultaneous opposing POV from chronological action. Temporal cuts must not read as cloned characters or objects in one continuous space.
- Each action folio has a dominant image; subsidiary views earn their space. Avoid identical grids, mandatory equal splits and three strips on every page.
- Canon and exact factual lettering outrank novelty. Use each character’s current reference assets in every shot, preserve morphology, costume, companions and named location geometry.
- Preserve full publication copy once and in source order. Distribute it deliberately through the visual reading path; no additional mandatory caption below each image, no extra invented dialogue.
- A quiet master image and a level documentary table are intentional storytelling choices. Do not panelize every supporting page or turn Opening Drive into six miniature matchup posters.
- Match meaningful objects, material states and weather between pages. Editorial visual echoes do not prove physical adjacency, contemporaneity or character travel.
- Inspect each native folio and 390px reading proof for POV legibility, coherent cause, readable text and exact score before visual acceptance. Tests verify instructions only, never artistic success.
The visual_storytelling shot plan defines all within-page views. Existing camera/foreground fields describe the anchor scene, not a ban on supplementary or opposing views. Do not copy old paragraph-strip placement from a precedent.

One individual 1080×1920 grayscale integrated illustrated memo folio: complete supplied headline, department heading, deck, narration and source-qualified lettering in the exact intended final composition. No annotation cards, miniature scene summaries, detached document exports, or generated six-up grids.

Image authority is explicit: current_appearance_override controls current face and costume; approved_lineup_crop supplies canonical identity; published_memo_precedent supplies only its stated layout/geometry application and never replaces current character anatomy or current scores. Follow the actual published memo references: one continuous cinematic world, prominent editorial hierarchy, slender ornament where appropriate, full narrative typography inside deliberate atmospheric reading space, and scene-led differences between departments. Grayscale affects palette, not the amount of copy, character identity, panel proportions, camera or final-page geometry.

SCENE-LED DESIGN RULES
- One complete 1080x1920 portrait folio may contain multiple deliberate camera views. The current visual_storytelling shot contract controls the sequence; a complete artwork is not limited to one shot.
- Preserve published typographic identity and department recognition; vary placement and scale with scene function.
- No default header/image/bottom-paragraph stack; typography and image are composed together.
- Keep complete source copy; compose space around it rather than cutting words to fit.
- Long prose is editorial typography, not implausibly engraved on tiny props.
- Use short in-world lettering only where physically plausible. Exact names, data, scores and tables are deterministically typeset, straight and legible.
- Identical department_heading and deck are displayed once; source fields are not interpreted as a command to repeat the same matchup line twice.
- Characters and props may cross decoration but never essential letters or data.
- Test full native page at 390px phone width; reflow the page rather than shrink the copy below readable scale.
- Canonical identity, appearance overrides, established geometry and verified final sports facts take precedence over composition novelty.
- All new personal quotes, jokes, anecdotes and factual claims require source/Founder input; an AI suggestion is not accepted canon.

SEQUENTIAL VISUAL STORYTELLING
- One complete 1080x1920 folio is the output artifact, not a rule requiring one camera view. Multiple shots/POV areas within that folio are explicitly authorized working direction.
- Use a visual sequence only when the additional view reveals new information: intention, cause, response or consequence. Do not pad with duplicate crops of the same pose.
- Distinguish simultaneous opposing POV from chronological action. Temporal cuts must not read as cloned characters or objects in one continuous space.
- Each action folio has a dominant image; subsidiary views earn their space. Avoid identical grids, mandatory equal splits and three strips on every page.
- Canon and exact factual lettering outrank novelty. Use each character’s current reference assets in every shot, preserve morphology, costume, companions and named location geometry.
- Preserve full publication copy once and in source order. Distribute it deliberately through the visual reading path; no additional mandatory caption below each image, no extra invented dialogue.
- A quiet master image and a level documentary table are intentional storytelling choices. Do not panelize every supporting page or turn Opening Drive into six miniature matchup posters.
- Match meaningful objects, material states and weather between pages. Editorial visual echoes do not prove physical adjacency, contemporaneity or character travel.
- Inspect each native folio and 390px reading proof for POV legibility, coherent cause, readable text and exact score before visual acceptance. Tests verify instructions only, never artistic success.
The visual_storytelling shot plan defines all within-page views. Existing camera/foreground fields describe the anchor scene, not a ban on supplementary or opposing views. Do not copy old paragraph-strip placement from a precedent.

## W10-P27 — TWELVE TEAMS. TEN POSTED RESULTS.

One individual 1080×1920 grayscale integrated illustrated memo folio: complete supplied headline, department heading, deck, narration and source-qualified lettering in the exact intended final composition. No annotation cards, miniature scene summaries, detached document exports, or generated six-up grids.

Image authority is explicit: current_appearance_override controls current face and costume; approved_lineup_crop supplies canonical identity; published_memo_precedent supplies only its stated layout/geometry application and never replaces current character anatomy or current scores. Follow the actual published memo references: one continuous cinematic world, prominent editorial hierarchy, slender ornament where appropriate, full narrative typography inside deliberate atmospheric reading space, and scene-led differences between departments. Grayscale affects palette, not the amount of copy, character identity, panel proportions, camera or final-page geometry.

SCENE-LED DESIGN RULES
- One complete 1080x1920 portrait folio may contain multiple deliberate camera views. The current visual_storytelling shot contract controls the sequence; a complete artwork is not limited to one shot.
- Preserve published typographic identity and department recognition; vary placement and scale with scene function.
- No default header/image/bottom-paragraph stack; typography and image are composed together.
- Keep complete source copy; compose space around it rather than cutting words to fit.
- Long prose is editorial typography, not implausibly engraved on tiny props.
- Use short in-world lettering only where physically plausible. Exact names, data, scores and tables are deterministically typeset, straight and legible.
- Identical department_heading and deck are displayed once; source fields are not interpreted as a command to repeat the same matchup line twice.
- Characters and props may cross decoration but never essential letters or data.
- Test full native page at 390px phone width; reflow the page rather than shrink the copy below readable scale.
- Canonical identity, appearance overrides, established geometry and verified final sports facts take precedence over composition novelty.
- All new personal quotes, jokes, anecdotes and factual claims require source/Founder input; an AI suggestion is not accepted canon.

SEQUENTIAL VISUAL STORYTELLING
- One complete 1080x1920 folio is the output artifact, not a rule requiring one camera view. Multiple shots/POV areas within that folio are explicitly authorized working direction.
- Use a visual sequence only when the additional view reveals new information: intention, cause, response or consequence. Do not pad with duplicate crops of the same pose.
- Distinguish simultaneous opposing POV from chronological action. Temporal cuts must not read as cloned characters or objects in one continuous space.
- Each action folio has a dominant image; subsidiary views earn their space. Avoid identical grids, mandatory equal splits and three strips on every page.
- Canon and exact factual lettering outrank novelty. Use each character’s current reference assets in every shot, preserve morphology, costume, companions and named location geometry.
- Preserve full publication copy once and in source order. Distribute it deliberately through the visual reading path; no additional mandatory caption below each image, no extra invented dialogue.
- A quiet master image and a level documentary table are intentional storytelling choices. Do not panelize every supporting page or turn Opening Drive into six miniature matchup posters.
- Match meaningful objects, material states and weather between pages. Editorial visual echoes do not prove physical adjacency, contemporaneity or character travel.
- Inspect each native folio and 390px reading proof for POV legibility, coherent cause, readable text and exact score before visual acceptance. Tests verify instructions only, never artistic success.
The visual_storytelling shot plan defines all within-page views. Existing camera/foreground fields describe the anchor scene, not a ban on supplementary or opposing views. Do not copy old paragraph-strip placement from a precedent.

## W10-P28 — THREE DIVISIONS. DIFFERENT PRESSURES.

One individual 1080×1920 grayscale integrated illustrated memo folio: complete supplied headline, department heading, deck, narration and source-qualified lettering in the exact intended final composition. No annotation cards, miniature scene summaries, detached document exports, or generated six-up grids.

Image authority is explicit: current_appearance_override controls current face and costume; approved_lineup_crop supplies canonical identity; published_memo_precedent supplies only its stated layout/geometry application and never replaces current character anatomy or current scores. Follow the actual published memo references: one continuous cinematic world, prominent editorial hierarchy, slender ornament where appropriate, full narrative typography inside deliberate atmospheric reading space, and scene-led differences between departments. Grayscale affects palette, not the amount of copy, character identity, panel proportions, camera or final-page geometry.

SCENE-LED DESIGN RULES
- One complete 1080x1920 portrait folio may contain multiple deliberate camera views. The current visual_storytelling shot contract controls the sequence; a complete artwork is not limited to one shot.
- Preserve published typographic identity and department recognition; vary placement and scale with scene function.
- No default header/image/bottom-paragraph stack; typography and image are composed together.
- Keep complete source copy; compose space around it rather than cutting words to fit.
- Long prose is editorial typography, not implausibly engraved on tiny props.
- Use short in-world lettering only where physically plausible. Exact names, data, scores and tables are deterministically typeset, straight and legible.
- Identical department_heading and deck are displayed once; source fields are not interpreted as a command to repeat the same matchup line twice.
- Characters and props may cross decoration but never essential letters or data.
- Test full native page at 390px phone width; reflow the page rather than shrink the copy below readable scale.
- Canonical identity, appearance overrides, established geometry and verified final sports facts take precedence over composition novelty.
- All new personal quotes, jokes, anecdotes and factual claims require source/Founder input; an AI suggestion is not accepted canon.

SEQUENTIAL VISUAL STORYTELLING
- One complete 1080x1920 folio is the output artifact, not a rule requiring one camera view. Multiple shots/POV areas within that folio are explicitly authorized working direction.
- Use a visual sequence only when the additional view reveals new information: intention, cause, response or consequence. Do not pad with duplicate crops of the same pose.
- Distinguish simultaneous opposing POV from chronological action. Temporal cuts must not read as cloned characters or objects in one continuous space.
- Each action folio has a dominant image; subsidiary views earn their space. Avoid identical grids, mandatory equal splits and three strips on every page.
- Canon and exact factual lettering outrank novelty. Use each character’s current reference assets in every shot, preserve morphology, costume, companions and named location geometry.
- Preserve full publication copy once and in source order. Distribute it deliberately through the visual reading path; no additional mandatory caption below each image, no extra invented dialogue.
- A quiet master image and a level documentary table are intentional storytelling choices. Do not panelize every supporting page or turn Opening Drive into six miniature matchup posters.
- Match meaningful objects, material states and weather between pages. Editorial visual echoes do not prove physical adjacency, contemporaneity or character travel.
- Inspect each native folio and 390px reading proof for POV legibility, coherent cause, readable text and exact score before visual acceptance. Tests verify instructions only, never artistic success.
The visual_storytelling shot plan defines all within-page views. Existing camera/foreground fields describe the anchor scene, not a ban on supplementary or opposing views. Do not copy old paragraph-strip placement from a precedent.
